## v0.148 bounded next-original-ID recovery

`BrokerRequestJournal.lookup_review(evaluated_at=...)` passively replays one bounded snapshot and selects the earliest retained due unresolved request, then least attempts/client ID as deterministic ties. It skips prepared-unsent, terminal and attempt-capped requests without deleting or rearming them; skipped terminal/capped history still needs separate review. Account pending lookup, authentication/review block, clock regression, shared lookup cooldown or insufficient aggregate recovery-event headroom prevents selection. Missing history creates no directories/files. Returned candidate/head/time/counts/capacity are diagnostics: lookup_admitted, admission_reserved, automatic continuation/retry/submission and financial/release flags remain false.

`nexus.paper_recovery_step.recover_next` and `recover_next_saved` perform one separately confirmed original-ID lookup through the existing fixed paper GET coordinator. The exact selection head is checked against read-only original terms before provider IO and again under SQLite write ownership by the optional expected_head on begin_lookup_checked. A concurrent append refuses before a provider request rather than silently choosing another target. There is no scan loop, automatic retry, submission/cancellation, callback-driven financial authority or new application path/URL input. Saved reads retain credential-epoch/final-OS-pair checks, clock continuity and optional explicit account pacing. A failure may already retain pending evidence; never automatically retry, erase it or infer no fill. Legacy callers omitting the new head reference keep existing callback/crash-recovery semantics; event schema, original IDs, caps and replay are unchanged.

The explicit source CLI is `python -m nexus.paper_recovery_step --expected-account UUID --journal PRIVATE_REQUEST_JOURNAL --pacing PRIVATE_PACING_JOURNAL --review-only`. It reads local selection/pacing without opening the vault/provider and preserves missing storage. For one confirmed saved-Windows-key read, replace --review-only with --confirm-read-only-paper. Both paths must be distinct and outside the checkout; original request history must already exist and be eligible. Review may show a local candidate while the separately reported pacing state still blocks admission. No key/secret arguments, environment-key fallback, default journal, recurring schedule or automatically started worker is added. Output includes private client IDs/head/account scope; keep it outside Git. Fixed CLI usage/failure messages never echo arguments, paths or transport details. Public artifact rules reject standalone selection, review and recovery-step JSON even when renamed.

The existing Accounts offline recovery check now demonstrates two separately invoked next-request steps, each with one original-ID lookup, plus passive cooldown preservation and a changed-head denial before IO. UI validates the fixture's checks and false authority flags, clears failed/stale results and uses the existing practical black/purple help. The mandatory pacing release profile requires all selection, single-step, cooldown/no-write and head-fence evidence, without changing its original pacing/submission tests or the sixteen-profile count. All fixture account/HTTPS/vault data is injected or temporary; no actual provider/vault is accessed by runtime fixtures. Unit checks include fairness, skipped states, pending/auth/rate/clock/headroom blocks before vault, persistent attempt caps, terminal observation, in-flight head changes, interrupted IO, lost result return, final saved-key readback, explicit consent and actual CLI missing-state/privacy behavior.

## v0.148.1 shared recovery review and process-death trials

`paper_recovery_step.review_next` composes bounded read-only request and optional exact-account pacing snapshots, then samples both again to reject changes during composition. It reports pending/authentication-or-review/clock/cooldown/session-cap blocks before saved-key access. Both explicit recovery entry points use this preflight; separately confirmed reads still recheck keys, time, local OS ownership, pacing admission and the exact request head under write ownership. Local clear checks do not reserve either journal or grant provider/trading permission; sequential snapshots cannot establish an atomic cross-database state or detect valid-tail/ABA replacement. Missing histories stay absent. The existing private --review-only CLI now includes these combined reasons while preserving its selection/pacing fields. Actual output contains private journal/account scope and must remain outside Git.

`python -m nexus.paper_recovery_drill` accepts no production storage or credentials and creates five disposable treatments: death after inserting lookup begin before commit, after begin commit, after inserting lookup result before commit, after result commit before return/pacing completion, and after pacing-result commit before return. Each uses injected GET-only account/original-ID/account responses and two fresh-process reviews. The drill requires exact retained attempts, partial-fill observation only after result commit, preserved original submission uncertainty, the expected pending/completed pacing state, real precommit rollback sidecars, unchanged fingerprints of both SQLite main files and every rollback/WAL/shared-memory sidecar, reacquired kernel ownership and no automatic follow-up. Pending request or pacing state and completed-session cooldown must deny a further explicit saved-key step before vault/provider access; released OS ownership never clears retained pending evidence.

The existing sixteen-profile suite now requires all five cases and all false authority flags in its pacing profile. Public drill output contains aggregate fixture counts/checks only; child errors expose fixed codes, not paths, raw stderr or keys. Hooks are local disposable-instance replacements in child processes and never production callbacks, startup work, a background recovery worker or order transport. This validates local commit/replay behavior, not actual provider outcomes, broker-wide pacing, native Windows/profile/ACL acceptance, source/fee/hold/funds reconciliation, sustained strategy performance, funding/live/cloud or independent/owner1.0 acceptance. ## v0.151 request-journal-derived paper activity collection

The explicit private `paper_activity_acceptance_probe` now accepts either the existing --orders PRIVATE_JSON input or --request-journal PRIVATE_REQUESTS plus --request-head SHA. In request mode it derives only allowlisted observed broker/client/symbol/side/status/quantity/average/time fields from marked original requests under the exact replayed head. Prepared-unsent records are skipped; any marked request without an observation, pending lookup, retained review block, missing/foreign/corrupt history, stale reference or destination alias refuses before vault/provider access. At least one observed original order is required. These retained observations are not independently authenticated provider data. The CLI still requires expected account, three distinct private destination journals, declared closed after/until window and --confirm-read-only-paper. Status-only rejects all collection inputs and remains vault/network silent. No manual order file or temporary copy is created in request mode.

The internal saved collector accepts an optional local input guard, checked before key capture, through reader permission/clock checkpoints, during retention admission and before/after its return. Request mode also checks before acceptance admission and via a new optional acceptance-store precommit_check immediately after insertion/replay before commit. Legacy callers retain their existing admission/postcommit sample behavior; no schema, receipt profile, retry, caps or default paths change. A changed source after a commit loses the return without deleting the receipt/acceptance record, releasing pending state, resetting attempts or automatically retrying. Request-head references are sampled diagnostics in the private report; they are not newly persisted in the existing acceptance record. Activity content and derived order terms remain bound by their existing normalization/receipt digests; v0.149/v0.150 reviews separately bind the requested current head. Sequential databases are not atomic or distributed ownership, and no hashes attest source or exclude valid-tail/ABA changes. Interrupted initial database creation can retain an uninitialized file requiring review; never erase it to bypass recovery.

The mandatory collection profile now uses injected saved-key/HTTPS seams in its isolated release child, derives one original order/two fills without an orders file, replays its accepted receipt and proves stale-head denial before further vault/GET access. Tests cover six mid-read/retention/acceptance/lost-return boundaries, prepared/unobserved/pending/authentication/foreign/stale/missing inputs, alias checks, source CLI and strict boolean precommit refusal. Runtime APIs, UI buttons and startup do not trigger this source-only workflow; all broker/source/fee/hold/funds/routing/live/release authority stays false. ## v0.152 durable request-origin binding and historical replay

New request-derived collections use acceptance record profile `nexus.paper-activity-acceptance-record.v3`, adding exact request_head_sha256 and request_orders_sha256 fields to the existing account/content/receipt/pacing/activity references. The order digest covers the sorted canonical order map and account digest. The same source guards and optional precommit check remain required. Existing file-input collections stay v2; the new reader accepts strict v2/v3 records in the same retained chain without migration or rewriting. Older readers reject v3 instead of ignoring fields; use the matching/newer source for these private histories. Storage/event/record caps, source request schema and receipt schema are unchanged. No profiles grant broker source, fill/fee/hold/funds, order or release authority.

`BrokerRequestJournal.observations_at_heads` accepts at most128 digest references, opens existing storage read-only, starts one SQLite read transaction and validates the entire bounded current journal before reconstructing requested historical observations. A corrupt later event refuses even if an earlier requested prefix was valid. Missing/unknown heads are explicit, returned state is detached, and absent history remains absent. Prefix output contains private original IDs and has its own public-artifact rejection mode. It cannot attest provider source, prevent valid-tail/ABA replacement or establish distributed/atomic cross-journal ownership. No history is truncated, repaired, rearmed or rewritten.

The accepted original-request/activity review now re-derives every v3 origin from its recorded historical prefix and compares canonical original orders and the exact retained receipt to the persisted order digest. Current request-head checks still apply separately; a later prepared request can leave current agreement blocked while the earlier collection's origin remains replayable. The combined account-evidence review exposes bound, legacy-unbound and replayed-prefix counts. Old v2 history remains usable for its previous local-consistency scope, with zero inferred request origins; mixed history cannot claim all origins are bound. Three-journal status replays the persisted order-input digest but explicitly leaves request_origin_history_verified false because no request history was supplied to that status function. A digest or consistent local rewrite is not a provider signature or proof of collection causality.

The mandatory collection release profile now requires persisted origin heads, historical-prefix replay after a later append and network/vault-silent fresh-process origin replay. Tests include missing/unobserved prefixes, rehashed substituted references/digests, corrupt suffixes, bounded invalid reference sets, detached state, missing/foreign histories, strict v3 shapes, mixed v2/v3 records and cold replay without storage changes. The new-source saved offline report must be regenerated; prior reports lack these mandatory checks and cannot certify this source. Runtime UI, startup and order routes are unchanged. ## v0.154 explicit packaged paper tools

New v0.154+ builds accept `NexusOperative.exe --paper-tool list` (or --help) for the three-command catalogue. `--paper-tool activity` invokes the existing explicit activity/status CLI, `--paper-tool account-review` the private combined evidence review, and `--paper-tool recovery` the single-step recovery/review CLI. Add --help after a command for its existing required arguments. The same source entry works as `python portable/desktop.py --paper-tool ...`. These operations dispatch before configure, app/uvicorn import, data-directory creation, sockets, server threads or UI. Unknown commands/extra top-level arguments fail with a fixed code, never desktop fallback. Child CLI SystemExit/return codes are preserved and sys.argv is restored. The frozen path never adds the installation folder to Python imports. No arbitrary module, Python -c, drills or subprocess-based release suite is exposed through this allowlist. Plain EXE launch remains the existing desktop workflow.

Commands retain their existing explicit paper-read confirmation, saved Windows-pair checks, exact account/head/current-epoch guards, durable pacing and bounded private histories. Read-only review/status requests no key/provider access. Frozen private paths additionally reject the EXE's own folder and resolved aliases; use a separate private user-data folder. Encrypted review inputs still decrypt in memory after private prompts; command output remains private plaintext, suitable only for an explicitly chosen private output destination. This is an operator acceptance path, not a required first-run settings chore or permission to send financial orders. ## v0.158 institutional design and execution-quality foundation

User direction now explicitly includes studying public institutional mechanics: Bloomberg AIM/PORT/EMSX, FlexTRADER, State Street Charles River, Iress, Itiviti/Tbricks, quantitative funds/HFT and IBKR. Read INSTITUTIONAL_DESIGN.md before choosing the next architecture change. That dated document preserves primary-source links, a platform-to-Nexus map, a practical ordered sequence and QuickFIX/SBE/CppTrader C++ candidates with their reviewed license distinctions. No proprietary source, private fund data, copied implementation or new dependency was imported. Avoid a wholesale C++ rewrite before profiling; preserve licensing notices and pin/review exact upstream revisions before any future adoption.

The target is parallel bounded council research outside a deterministic execution loop, expiring reviewed policy proposals, independent account-wide risk/stop/reservation checks, one execution owner per account, durable event-driven lifecycle and execution-cost feedback. Current learned council output remains advisory, current fixed-policy virtual execution unchanged and broker order routing disconnected. Do not evade provider limits with multiple clients or describe institution-style software as institution-free market access. Nanosecond timestamp precision is not clock synchronization, measured brokerage latency or a promise of nanosecond fills. More trades do not prove positive net edge or exponential growth.

Original nexus/execution_quality.py now supplies a bounded pure offline BTC/USD review: canonical unique execution IDs, explicit decimal prices/quantities/USD fee debits or None, same-domain-assumed integer nanoseconds, decision-benchmark gross shortfall, delay/arrival-spread/post-arrival-residual decomposition, supplied-fee totals/bps and separate unfilled end-mark opportunity cost. Positive signed shortfall is worse than the benchmark; a negative value is not realized profit. Missing fees stay unknown; a zero supplied fee never attests actual coverage. Missing end marks leave partial-order opportunity unknown; no-fill cases do not become successful zero-cost executions. Rebates/non-USD fees, causal impact, queue position, actual clock domains, fee/fill/source coverage and broker/account/order bindings remain unsupported/unverified. The function performs no network/vault/file/journal/ledger IO or routing. Decimal arithmetic uses a bounded local96-digit context and fixed rounding, not caller-selected rounding or float timestamps.

Matching records deduplicate without changing normalized input identity; conflicts, unsupported scopes, overfills, crossed quotes, out-of-interval fills and malformed/unbounded types refuse with fixed codes. The module self-test prints only synthetic aggregate checks. Detailed review output with real amounts is private; public_repo_audit rejects its report mode even if renamed. The capacity release profile now additionally requires the separately untimed synthetic execution-quality fixture and every authority flag false. Timed benchmark treatment scopes are unchanged. The read-only roadmap/API exposes the institutional strategy and remaining work; no PORTAL control was added, so existing beginner tabs and black/bright-purple help remain unchanged.

Next: bind this arithmetic to explicitly accepted exact-head paper-account/fill/fee and contemporaneous quote evidence, validate event-driven adapters and sequence recovery, introduce cost-aware proposals/shared reservations, compare eligible routes after costs, measure full-path tail latency and only then assess native acceleration. ## v0.159 exact-head retained paper execution-quality review

`nexus/paper_execution_quality_review.py` now derives the selected original request quantity/side and every retained explicit priced fill directly from locally accepted request/activity/pacing/acceptance histories. It reuses complete accepted-history replay, original individual limit/quantity checks and request-origin handling, checks the exact supplied current request/activity heads and common account, then repeats accepted replay and snapshots after arithmetic. A changed history fails without retry/repair. Selected client/broker/payload identities, quote-benchmark digest, exact cooperating heads and quality-input digest are bound in the private report. REST activity IDs are explicitly local surrogate identities, never claimed to be verified streaming execution IDs. No fill is priced from cumulative averages, and no fill is silently excluded to fit a favorable benchmark interval.

The bounded benchmark schema has exactly decision_mid, decision_at_ns, arrival_bid, arrival_ask, arrival_at_ns, end_at_ns and unfilled_mark. Decimal prices/mark are strings; nanosecond instants must be bounded integers. Quotes, clock domains and original request-event timing remain supplied/unverified. Canonical retained RFC3339 fill times are converted by integer calendar arithmetic, retaining nanoseconds/offset equivalence without float timestamps. Every fee input is None: retained fee rows are counted but never associated by a date/reference/residual match. Explicit fees and all fee-inclusive/whole-order totals stay unknown. No complete received coverage, funds, account, benchmark, causal impact, profit, latency or routing acceptance follows.

`NexusOperative.exe --paper-tool quality-review --help` is the sixth fixed packaged command (v0.159+). The CLI requires the private account ID, four distinct journals, exact current request/activity fingerprints, original client order ID and a fifth distinct private benchmark file. It rejects source/install paths and aliases before reads; benchmark JSON is bounded to4096bytes, refuses duplicate keys/nonfinite tokens, and is read again before acknowledgment. Private output contains identifiers, hashes and amounts and must stay outside Git. No password/key/provider access, default-data/desktop startup, journal/ledger writes or automatic continuation is added. This is optional operator tooling, not another first-run setup checkbox.

Standalone/installed smoke now executes nine child commands across six allowlisted tools and requires the original-request-derived quality result, unknown fees, false authority flags and unchanged private files. The existing capacity release profile additionally requires a separate synthetic accepted-history quality fixture; it is not included in timed benchmark treatments. Public artifact scanning rejects the new detailed financial-report mode. Tests cover stale heads, foreign/missing requests/accounts, missing/aliased histories, full-fill selection across a too-short benchmark, original individual-limit violations, retained concurrent request append, promoted/missing component authority or invented fee totals, exact RFC3339 nanoseconds, changed benchmark files, malformed/oversized/duplicate/nonfinite benchmark JSON and fixed-error CLI privacy. No PORTAL control changed; preserve beginner tabs and black/bright-purple help.

Actual authorized account/profile/source/fees and independent benchmark capture/time acceptance remain next; broad institutional event-driven routing, portfolio reservations and native C++ acceleration remain future work under INSTITUTIONAL_DESIGN.md. ## v0.160 explicit fixed-host BTC quote observation foundation

The internal `nexus/paper_quote_observation.py` reader makes exactly three GETs after separate explicit confirmation: paper account, `https://data.alpaca.markets/v1beta3/crypto/us/latest/quotes?symbols=BTC%2FUSD`, then the same paper account. The generic existing paper get(url) allowlist remains unchanged; only a no-URL-argument method admits this one market-data endpoint. Both paths share the existing redirect-disabled, exact response-URL, bounded body/header/time, permission/clock and <=4-GET session transport. Shared-transport refactoring preserves account/activity/asset/recovery guards. No live trading host, arbitrary URL/location/symbol, order method, retries or new network dependency is admitted.

Official documentation reviewed2026-10-18: https://docs.alpaca.markets/us/docs/authentication confirms paper-account access to the data.alpaca.markets host; https://docs.alpaca.markets/us/reference/cryptolatestquotes-1 documents the latest-quote endpoint and locations; https://docs.alpaca.markets/us/docs/real-time-crypto-pricing-data documents crypto quote fields and RFC3339 nanosecond timestamps. These public specifications are not actual account/schema/entitlement acceptance. Only fixed Alpaca US BTC/USD quote fields are admitted; foreign symbols, unknown fields, crossed prices, malformed sizes, stale/future timestamps and account disagreement refuse. Zero reported size stays review-only, not executable liquidity. Complete request/schema/source/location acceptance remains open.

The new reader requires a caller-selected private account-bound PaperReadPacing journal. Source/install paths are rejected before storage/keys. Successful reads share the retained60-second cooldown with inventory, activity, asset and recovery operations using that exact journal. Failed malformed reads preserve pending uncertainty; HTTP/rate/auth outcomes retain existing conservative rules. This is an explicit acceptance diagnostic, not a high-frequency polling feed or broker-wide/distributed quota proof. Existing public discovery/research refresh settings are unchanged.

Exchange timestamps retain all nine fractional digits; their integer-nanosecond representation is serialized as decimal text to avoid JavaScript number rounding. Local receipt/complete/finalized samples remain float seconds, explicitly not synchronized nanosecond clocks or actual order arrival. Freshness is rechecked after the final account GET and after durable pacing completion; a delayed commit cannot acknowledge a stale quote. Matching normalized account fields across reads are sequential stability only, not an atomic account/quote snapshot or entitlement proof.

`paper_credentials.inspect_quote_saved` is internal and separately confirmed. Invalid/corrupt/foreign/private-path scopes and retained pacing blocks are checked before vault access; actual admission is rechecked under existing ownership. Saved-key mutation epochs fence every IO checkpoint, and final OS-store readback suppresses return if another process replaces credentials. Revoked/incomplete reads preserve pending history; a completed but revoked/stale return never clears or rewrites its committed pacing result. No credentials/raw responses/private account fields are returned.

This batch adds no UI control, API route or EXE command, no default storage/startup network read, no automatic quote retention and no original request/decision/arrival-event benchmark binding. The output remains private account-bound financial data, with source/clock/benchmark/fees/venue/liquidity/funds/routing/release flags false; public artifact scanning rejects its report mode. Existing beginner tabs and black/bright-purple help remain unchanged. A synthetic reader+pacing fixture is mandatory in the inspection_inventory profile, with no actual provider/vault access or private report retained in release evidence.

## v0.161 private original-request quote retention

`nexus.paper_quote_store.PaperQuoteStore` is an internal offline foundation, with no default path, network/vault call, UI/API/EXE entry point or startup capture. It accepts a supplied v0.160-shaped observation and binds only its allowlisted normalized scope to an exact original request head, original client ID, retained plan digest and payload digest. The source label is copied unverified; even a valid hash does not prove the reader was called or that the quote was contemporaneous with a decision/order event. No supplied observation can promote any source, clock, benchmark, fee, funds, routing, money or release authority flag. All remain false. No execution-quality benchmark is generated or accepted automatically.

Both files must be distinct private paths outside the checkout/install tree. SQLite uses regular single-link bounded DELETE-mode files with WAL/sidecar rejection, FULL-synchronous transactions, a4MiB page/file cap,128 retained observations and8KiB record bounds. Public JSON artifact checks reject both history and receipt modes. Files are private financial information, not encrypted by this store; use an appropriate encrypted private volume and independently accept native profile/ACL/key recovery. Never publish reports, copy into Git, prune, rebaseline, convert WAL or clear history to regain admission.

Every review validates the complete current original-request tail and complete quote chain. Each retained request head must be a validated historical prefix containing that original PREPARE; immutable retained plan/payload digests must agree. Later request appends preserve earlier quote bindings. Capture time remains sampled local float seconds; source exchange nanoseconds are exact decimal text. Record replay reproduces normalization/freshness against the old sampled read times only: an old stored quote never becomes current executable data. No clock-domain synchronization, request-event causality, actual order arrival, venue/liquidity entitlement or broker latency is inferred.

Append requires explicit current request and quote heads, replays again under the quote write transaction, and checks original history and caller revocation before commit. Exact duplicates reuse identity without adding rows, including at capacity. Cap exhaustion refuses without eviction. Request change or revocation after commit suppresses acknowledgment but retains the committed record for explicit review. Separate SQLite files are sampled, not atomic; hashes do not authenticate data, exclude valid-tail deletion/ABA/path races, prove distributed ownership or reserve execution. Missing status reads remain absent. Failed first creation can leave an uninitialized file requiring review; no automatic repair is provided.

The new store is deliberately not a collection coordinator and does not consume or change any pacing journal. ## v0.162 explicit quote capture and offline recovery

`nexus.paper_quote_capture` adds the seventh allowlisted EXE tool, `--paper-tool quote-capture`. Normal no-argument desktop startup is unchanged. Status needs no saved keys, network, directory/database creation or repair. A read requires separate `--confirm-read-only-paper`, an existing original request/client ID, exact current request/quote/pacing heads and three distinct private paths outside source/install directories. It makes the existing three fixed GETs only; it never prepares, marks, submits, cancels or retries an order. No bank/funding/live or Engage authority follows.

Preflight replays complete histories and checks original terms, caps and cooldown before touching saved credentials. The reader rechecks the expected pacing head after acquiring its local read lease and before reserving/IO. Existing redirect, fixed-host/body/time, account, clock and saved-key mutation safeguards remain. After the reader completes its durable result, capture reacquires pacing ownership, rejects any intervening transition, and holds ownership through quote retention/cleanup. This is a checked handoff, not an uninterrupted lease or distributed reservation. Uncooperating writers, ABA/path races and other hosts remain outside the local contract.

Existing supplied v1 quotes remain replayable without rewriting. New capture records use v2 with the selected local pacing-path/account scope digest and exact before/after heads. Offline review requires precisely one v2 record for every retained successful pacing session in order, plus original-request historical-prefix agreement. Supplied v1 records, pending/failed/interrupted/unbound sessions, wrong copied-path scope or mismatched transitions block further capture. Never invent a missing receipt, clear uncertainty, delete/rebaseline history or switch journals to bypass review/provider limits. Captures do not belong in the existing accepted-activity pacing history: its one-activity-receipt-per-session contract remains unchanged. A reviewed typed central budget is still required for mixed operations; a dedicated diagnostic path is not proof of provider-wide quota coordination.

The saved-pair consumer keeps the captured epoch and repeated OS-store readback checks inside the credential module through local retention. It shares a permission guard for original/quote history during the read, uses post-lock checks before quote commit, and suppresses acknowledgment after key/history revocation while preserving already committed evidence. A completed provider read followed by failed/no quote retention stays explicitly unbound; a committed quote with lost return remains reviewable. Full process-death fixtures cover both outcomes. This is local content agreement, never provider causality or source attestation.

Capture stores the reader's sampled timing; historical replay never makes an old quote fresh for execution. Exact exchange nanoseconds and a recent latest quote do not establish a decision/arrival event, clock synchronization, source/location entitlement, executable liquidity, fees, latency, spendable funds or profitability. No benchmark is generated/promoted automatically. All authority flags remain false. Reports and journals are private financial information; the store itself is not encryption. Native private-volume/ACL/key recovery, actual source/schema/account acceptance and reviewed event timing remain required.

The common standalone/installed smoke now runs eleven actual child commands across seven tools, adding a bound synthetic quote-status replay and refusal of an unconfirmed quote read. All disposable fixture files are prepared before byte-snapshot comparison; child private JSON/paths stay out of aggregate evidence. The inspection_inventory profile requires actual coordinator/reader/retention fixture checks with injected transport and no actual provider/vault access. This is not actual Windows/provider acceptance. Existing beginner tabs/help, engine/throttle and disconnected order adapters remain unchanged.

Next: accept this explicit flow on separately authorized paper-account/native-profile data; review source/location/clock domains and original event times; integrate typed provider-wide budgeting before mixed production collection. Connected broker-paper, sustained market, verified fees/holds/funds, secure funding/live/cloud, independent security/risk and owner1.0 gates remain open. Rough implementation remains about40% (30–45%, low confidence),0/9 fully accepted major stages.

## v0.163 encrypted portable full-chain quote evidence

The eighth allowlisted EXE tool, `--paper-tool quote-evidence`, provides explicit `seal`, `review` and aggregate `self-test` actions. It never reads broker keys, contacts a provider, starts the desktop/research/trading engine, restores/imports journals or grants permissions. This optional diagnostic supports private review on another machine without transferring credentials or writing a decrypted database/bundle. Existing no-argument startup, beginner help and order disconnection remain unchanged.

Seal requires one or more locally bound captured quotes, distinct private source paths and exact current request/quote/pacing heads. It snapshots all bounded original-request events, quote records and pacing events using read-only transactions, validates every chain and historical original-request prefix, and rechecks current source heads/content. The portable account UUID, source-scope digest, selected heads and raw allowlisted event rows are private. The original local pacing-path scope is retained as a digest; offline review does not reinterpret it as this host's provider budget or authorize resumed capture. There is no import or path-rebinding function.

The new domain-separated `NEXUS-QUOTE-EVIDENCE-ENC-1` envelope uses AES256-GCM with authenticated header, random16-byte salt/12-byte nonce and the existing fixed scrypt N131072/r8/p1 policy. Passwords use hidden terminal prompts and confirmation for seal, are never command-line/environment options or persisted, and reject echoing fallbacks. Ciphertext is bounded by a20MiB canonical plaintext cap plus fixed header/tag overhead; row/event counts and bytes retain existing stricter journal bounds. Export refuses over-cap data rather than pruning, compressing away or rewriting evidence. No untrusted KDF/SQL/schema/path parameters are accepted. Password encryption protects copied bytes under that password; it does not authenticate the exporter, broker, source or currentness. Python does not promise erasure of secret strings in memory.

Review authenticates before returning plaintext, then replays all three copied journals in memory-only SQLite with memory temp storage and fixed schemas. Original full-tail replay, earlier request prefixes, immutable retained terms, quote normalization/source nanosecond text, exact heads and one quote per successful pacing transition must agree. Unknown/extra keys, duplicate/nonfinite/noncanonical JSON, mixed accounts, modified heads/tails, malformed/oversized rows and unbound/interrupted histories refuse. A matching GCM tag alone is insufficient. No plaintext file or SQLite database is written; private review JSON prints to stdout unless `--public-summary` selects fixed bounded integer counts and false authority flags. Redirecting private stdout is the operator's separate plaintext action. Public summary is unsigned aggregate content consistency, never acceptance/source proof.

Seal writes only a NEW selected encrypted file outside source/install paths, after source rechecks and password verification. Existing outputs, symlinks and source-file/rollback/WAL/shared-memory/owner-lock aliases refuse before prompting. The copy is flushed/read back and sources are checked again before acknowledgment. A failed/changed post-write check can leave a new partial or complete encrypted file with no success acknowledgment; originals and all prior copies remain untouched. Never delete/rewrite source history to regain export. The copy operation does not encrypt source databases, terminal logs, prior downloads or other copies; native private-volume/ACL/password recovery still needs acceptance.

Artifact audit rejects renamed ciphertext by magic, plaintext bundles by profile and detailed review reports by mode. No actual account material, ciphertext or password enters Git or aggregate trial reports. Mandatory inspection_inventory evidence exercises supplied temporary capture, later original-history append, full in-memory replay, actual authenticated-encryption roundtrip, randomized ciphertext, wrong-password/tamper rejection and unchanged source files. Eight tools receive thirteen actual source-entry child commands; standalone/installed workflows require the same smoke, now including the new in-package self-test and malformed export denial without prompts/keys. This remains disposable supplied-data evidence, not actual provider/native acceptance.

Next: accept the explicit capture/export/review flow on authorized native paper-account history; independently review provider schema/location/source, event/clock domains and fee links, then integrate a typed central budget before mixed production collection. Encryption/full replay cannot turn a latest quote into a verified decision/arrival benchmark or enable routing. Connected broker-paper, sustained market trials, verified fees/holds/funds, secure funding/live/cloud, independent security/risk and owner1.0 gates remain open. Rough implementation remains about40% (30–45%, low confidence),0/9 fully accepted major stages.

## v0.163.1 related private-path preflight

Quote capture now rejects a selected request, quote or pacing journal when it equals another selected journal's possible `-journal`, `-wal` or `-shm` file, or the pacing journal's `.owner.lock` file. This relationship check runs before vault access, provider IO or file creation, preventing a confirmed read from succeeding only to collide with its own private recovery/ownership files during retention. Existing exact-path, source/install-path, symlink, hard-link and SQLite-layout guards remain unchanged.

## v0.164 declared read purposes and fixed request sequences

Five standard readers now use fixed internal purposes: ACCOUNT_INVENTORY, BTC_ASSET_RULES, ACTIVITY_COLLECTION, ORIGINAL_ID_RECOVERY and BTC_QUOTE_OBSERVATION. Each enforces its own request-kind sequence in addition to the existing fixed URL/host/method, account/clock/credential and <=4-GET checks. Inventory cannot call the quote host mid-session; all operations start with account identity. Activity allows one/two bounded pages between account reads. Successful original-ID recovery requires a final account read; a retained non-success lookup may end after the original two GETs without a further request. A wrong sequence, incomplete scope or premature internal success cannot finalize a successful pacing result. Existing failure/pending/outcome behavior remains conservative.

When local pacing is selected, BEGIN now retains the reader's declared purpose only with a valid held ownership lease, inside the same hash-chained reservation committed before IO. New metadata adds no GET allowance: the existing60-second result cooldown, rate/auth/pending/interruption blocks,256-session and byte caps remain shared across all purposes. Switching purpose cannot bypass the same journal's budget. Unpaced legacy API options are not silently converted to production budgets; the standard transport still enforces their purpose sequence. No default file or startup/network work is added.

Read-only operation_history and pacing status expose static purpose counts, pending purpose and retained start/result/unknown/interruption metadata without credential, URL or order details. Older owned/unowned BEGIN records remain byte-for-byte unchanged and replay as LEGACY_UNSPECIFIED; no purpose is inferred from time, response or later receipts. New typed history requires v0.164+; older readers can refuse its unknown record shape. Current encrypted quote bundles retain these raw rows and replay both old untyped and new typed histories without migration/import. Exact copied-history validation is not broker/source/timing/currentness attestation.

Activity acceptance only matches successful ACTIVITY_COLLECTION or explicitly legacy-unspecified transitions; quote capture and encrypted quote review reject known non-quote purposes. Legacy remains unverified and is never relabeled. Existing complete-history/count/head/receipt checks remain in force: filtering eligible transition purposes does not ignore unrelated sessions or approve mixed histories. Do not put quote/inventory/asset/recovery sessions into an accepted activity journal and then delete, rebaseline or fabricate receipts. A separately reviewed central/provider-wide budget, complete mixed-operation receipt model and distributed ownership are still required before production integration. These declared local labels and OS leases do not authenticate a provider or prevent uncooperating writers, forged local hashes, valid-tail deletion/ABA or another host.

The mandatory pacing trial now additionally exercises all five actual readers with injected responses on one temporary journal, then an untyped legacy session, fresh-instance replay and denied cross-purpose read during the same retained cooldown. Public evidence retains aggregate booleans only. Tests cover fixed-purpose path/incomplete/premature-success refusals, owned marker requirements, malformed/changed metadata, legacy encrypted replay, wrong-purpose quote/activity receipts, process death retaining typed uncertainty and explicit interrupted recovery without clearing history. Eight EXE tools/thirteen child commands and beginner GUI behavior remain unchanged.

Next: accept native/account behavior, independently review declared purposes and central budget/receipt semantics, then source/clock/event/fee/hold/funds reconciliation. Actual broker-paper, sustained market, secure funding/live/cloud, independent security/risk and owner1.0 gates remain open. ## v0.165 retained checked-admission samples

Older untimed markers remain unchanged and report submission_admission=null; unmarked requests also report null alongside submission_attempt_marked=false. Full-chain encrypted quote copies retain and validate the new original-request rows while preserving older quote-prefix bindings. ## v0.165.1 explicit projection timestamp profile

The older supplied linked-fee projection now enforces its microsecond timestamp profile at the projection boundary for opening/closing times and every order, execution and fee row. Finer fractions, including zero-padded fractions beyond six digits, refuse rather than silently truncate. Unsupported/naive timestamp syntax, unknown -00:00 offsets and invalid offset components refuse. Valid supported timestamps normalize to UTC before duplicate comparisons, window checks and input hashing; equivalent offsets and precision spellings no longer create conflicting identities. Distinct supported microseconds still conflict for a reused execution ID and retain chronological checks. The combined supplied balance review shares this validator. Caps are still checked before row normalization.

The raw record helpers used by the separate REST adapter remain unchanged. REST activity/quote evidence continues retaining exact nanoseconds; this fix does not round or import it into the older projection. Cash-path review still groups supported timestamps conservatively at microsecond precision and requires all same-group debits to be prefunded. Unsupported precision now refuses instead of collapsing into a misleading same-time group. No finer execution ordering, spendable balance or provider chronology is inferred. Existing retained journals and previously published reports are not rewritten. UTC canonicalization can change the digest of earlier non-UTC supplied projection inputs; regenerate their private diagnostic instead of rewriting source history.

Mandatory balance evidence now checks all five precision-entry boundaries, semantic timezone deduplication/digest equality and unchanged exact REST nanoseconds using aggregate-only supplied fixtures. This adds no UI/control, account/key/provider IO, ledger writes, fee-link invention, routing or release authority. v0.165 admission samples, v0.164 read-purpose fences and v0.163.1 related-path checks remain intact. Actual broker-paper, central budget/mixed-receipt review, sustained market, source/clock/fee/hold/funds acceptance, secure funding/live/cloud, native-profile/ACL, independent and owner1.0 gates remain open. ## v0.165.2 shared private storage preflight

A common bounded read-only path-set guard now rejects selected database/input paths that equal another selected path or its possible SQLite -journal/-wal/-shm or .owner.lock companion. It also rejects normalized aliases and file/directory ancestor conflicts, including a selected path inside another journal's companion name. Unrelated suffix-bearing names remain allowed. The helper resolves at most eight caller-selected paths, creates no files and returns fixed failure codes without exposing private names.

The guard is shared by quote capture/export sources, direct and saved original-ID recovery, recovery selection, direct and saved activity collection, activity acceptance collection/status (including supplied-order or request-journal inputs), original request/activity acceptance, account-evidence review and execution-quality review. Saved readers check before capturing credentials; direct readers check before session construction/reservation; composed reviews check before journal replay. Existing exact-distinct errors where present, individual file/symlink/hardlink/sidecar checks, source/install protections, account scope, OS ownership, clock, cooldown and complete-history checks remain. Legacy optional unpaced APIs are not silently assigned default files or broker-wide budgets.

This extends the quote-only v0.163.1 correction without rewriting journals or repairing conflicts. Users must choose separate private locations and inspect existing evidence; preflight never deletes files, clears pending history or retries. The real-loopback paper HTTP trial now tolerates one platform-specific close only after it has verified an exact403 response and `Cache-Control: no-store` for a pre-credential foreign-origin denial. ## v0.166 exact original snapshot event ordering

Original order snapshots already retained raw updated_at text, but SnapshotTracker compared Python datetime values that discarded fractional digits beyond six. Distinct nanosecond updates within one microsecond could therefore falsely conflict. Comparisons now use integer nanoseconds for RFC3339 timestamps with up to nine fractional digits and explicit offsets. No floating-point epoch conversion is used. Backward updates still refuse, equal-instant conflicting observations still require review, and exact replay of the current observation remains idempotent. Quantity, identity, status-transition, terminal-state and capacity guards remain unchanged.

Normalized snapshots and durable request events retain the original timestamp spelling, so supported existing rows, hashes and quote-prefix references are not rewritten. Existing ISO spellings representable at microsecond precision remain supported. Unsupported finer profiles (more than nine digits, or non-RFC3339 fine fractions) now refuse instead of silently truncating; any old history using such a profile requires explicit review, never automatic repair or deletion. The separate older linked-fee projection remains restricted to its explicit microsecond profile.

Full journal replay uses the same comparison and can recover valid updates one nanosecond apart. Tests cover all one-to-nine-digit precisions, timezone/day-boundary equivalence, raw text retention, unchanged state after regression/conflict, fresh-process offline recovery and encrypted full-chain quote-copy replay with historical request prefixes. Mandatory pacing evidence includes aggregate-only supplied ordering checks and false authority flags.

This is event ordering within supplied timestamps, not trusted source time, synchronized decision/arrival clocks, a latency benchmark or permission for nanosecond market execution. No provider/key access, order route, fee inference, ledger credit, default connection or GUI control is added. Actual broker-paper, central budget/mixed-operation receipts, sustained market, source/clock/fee/hold/funds, secure funding/live/cloud, native-profile/ACL, independent and owner1.0 gates remain open. Rough implementation remains about40% (30–45%, low confidence),0/9 fully accepted major stages.

## v0.166.2 original read-lease completion

Typed pacing BEGIN required local ownership, but the outcome writer previously accepted a token without checking the original acquisition. Typed finish now requires the same PaperReadPacing object, owner object and still-active ownership acquisition that returned the reservation. A private in-memory acquisition marker changes on each successful lease entry. Completion checks held ownership, that marker and the retained owner scope before invoking caller admission and again after inserting the result, before commit. Standard read sessions pass their original owner explicitly.

A missing/released owner, reacquisition (including reuse of the same owner object), another gate instance or a restarted process cannot turn an uncertain typed reservation into a known result. Failed checks roll back the result and retain pending history. Lost return after a successful commit still leaves the result retained, never a safe new attempt. Cold recovery uses the existing explicit interruption review, retaining unknown outcome, session count and full-day cooldown; no timeout, automatic clearing or retry is introduced.

This is an in-process cooperating-caller contract alongside existing OS locking, not an unforgeable source proof or distributed lease. Private Python callers can alter memory; administrator/path races and other hosts remain outside the local guarantee. No acquisition nonce or new schema field is stored. Existing raw event rows, legacy untyped completion and encrypted full-chain replay remain compatible; prior typed RESULT rows are not retrospectively certified as uninterrupted ownership. All source, mixed-history, quota, broker, funds, routing and release authority remains false.

Mandatory pacing evidence checks original completion, reacquisition/cold refusal, unchanged uncertainty and explicit interruption. Tests cover every declared purpose, success/error/rate/auth/transport outcomes, post-insert release rollback, a fresh child process and lost commit acknowledgment. Eight EXE tools/thirteen child commands and beginner GUI behavior are unchanged. Actual broker-paper, central budget/mixed receipts, sustained market, source/clock/fee/hold/funds, secure funding/live/cloud, ordinary native-profile/ACL, independent and owner1.0 gates remain open. ## v0.167.0 content-free mixed-operation result receipts

Each successful typed fixed paper-read session now commits a receipt into the central pacing hash chain. The receipt binds the declared operation, exact fixed request-kind sequence, result outcome, both pacing heads, evaluation time and one SHA-256 digest per fully parsed response. Error outcomes bind the attempted request prefix without reading or retaining the failed response body. The journal stores no raw broker response, credential or plaintext account identifier.

All five real read paths produce receipts: account inventory, asset-rule inspection, activity collection, original-order lookup and BTC quote observation. Receipt validation occurs before commit under the original held read lease. A wrong operation, request sequence, digest or outcome fails closed and leaves the reservation pending. Fresh replay revalidates the receipt digest and sequence.

Earlier typed results remain readable but explicitly unreceipted and cannot satisfy activity, request/fill or reconciliation acceptance. Legacy results remain legacy and never receive synthetic receipts. No migration, deletion, retry or repair is automatic. These receipts prove local cooperating-code consistency only, not provider identity, completeness, broker-wide quotas, synchronized clocks, market realism, funds or profitability. No API/UI control was added, so the beginner tabs and black/bright-purple bottom-right help are unchanged. Permissions remain OFF and no provider, routing, funding, ledger or release authority is granted. Actual broker-paper, sustained-market, funding/live/cloud, native-profile/ACL, independent review and owner 1.0 gates remain open. The rough implementation estimate remains about 40% (30–45%, low confidence), with 0/9 fully accepted stages.

## v0.167.3 activity receipts match retained request scope

Full replay applies it to all accepted history, so a valid later tail cannot conceal an earlier mismatch.

The existing pacing_request_targets_bound/all_pacing_request_targets_bound fields now require that retained-scope comparison, rather than merely the v2 profile. Legacy/unreceipted/v1 classifications remain unchanged. Synthetic fixture targets now describe their actual declared windows instead of arbitrary digests. Existing published journals/reports are not migrated, relabeled, repaired, deleted or rewritten; inconsistent retained history requires review without an automatic retry or replacement used to conceal uncertainty.

Regression covers self-consistent wrong account endpoints, windows/cursors, unchanged history, blocked compositions, a later valid tail, cold vault/network-silent replay, one/two-page real injected collectors, every request position and exact second-page cursor spelling. Mandatory process-death evidence now also requires target-bound recovery and supplied changed-window/cursor refusal with full private file-set preservation. No new control/API/tool is added; the existing beginner tabs and black/bright-purple bottom-right help remain.

## v0.167.4 fixed endpoint receipt consistency

Fixed inventory, BTC asset-rule and BTC quote consumers previously required v2 target hashes without comparing their values to the exact approved GET endpoints. A self-consistent copied quote receipt containing the positions endpoint instead of the BTC quote endpoint still reported local agreement. This was a local consistency gap, not evidence of any actual provider call or unauthorized order.

A bounded pure fixed_targets_match helper now compares successful current-profile receipts to the complete ordered fixed GET target hashes and request-kind sequence. Dynamic activity and original-ID recovery are deliberately excluded; their targets require their own retained scope. Managed inventory and BTC rules adapters require that comparison plus the exact before/after pacing heads before returning an observation. Quote capture checks the new completed session under the reacquired ownership lease before retaining a quote, preserving any completed unbound read for review. Full local quote status checks all historical sessions; a mismatched tail cannot authorize another read or automatic retry.

Copied quote-bundle replay applies the same endpoint comparison in memory, including encrypted review. ## v0.168 normalized-result receipts across paper-read operations

Successful inventory, asset-rule, BTC quote, activity collection and original-ID recovery readers now attach a bounded normalized-result SHA256 to an explicit pacing receipt v3, alongside the original request-target and accepted raw-response byte digests. This is a co-reported cooperating-code content binding, not a cryptographic proof that bytes were normalized correctly, a trusted provider/source or verified coverage. No normalized plaintext, account identifier, cursor, order ID, key or response body is added to the pacing journal. Existing low-level v1/v2/untyped receipts remain readable, with normalized_result_bound false and result_projection_sha256 null; no synthetic fingerprint is inferred.

Inventory and asset fingerprints cover the complete normalized report before Portal wrapper fields are added. This includes inventory display fields, the portable export and open-order aggregates together. Managed consumers require both the existing exact local transition/endpoint checks and this result fingerprint. Quote fingerprints use the retained immutable observation scope; finalized_at is excluded because it is sampled after RESULT commits and remains separately freshness/clock-validated by quote replay. Quote capture refuses a different normalized observation before retention, full live quote status checks every capture, and copied/encrypted replay checks every retained quote against its paired operation. Receipt integrity, target agreement and normalized-result agreement remain distinct; any disagreement withholds local agreement/public agreement summaries without rewriting evidence.

Activity fingerprints bind the canonical collection receipt identity, which already includes normalized orders/activities, declared window and collection/page metadata. New acceptance admission and all historical status replay compare it, in addition to exact window/cursor target checks. Both request/fill and balance compositions explicitly require all_pacing_normalized_results_bound. A v2 or disagreeing v3 historical record yields ACCEPTED_NORMALIZED_RESULT_UNVERIFIED_REVIEW_REQUIRED even after a valid newer tail. Preserve those records and uncertainty; do not reset/retry or create a replacement journal to conceal history. Original-ID successful lookup receipts bind the selected ID and normalized journal result; non-success reads retain existing conservative v2 outcome/prefix/cooldown semantics and invent no normalized success. Full cross-operation/recovery acceptance remains open.

The session retains only a temporary reference to its already existing normalized result and rechecks its fingerprint after permission and clock callbacks through completion. A pre-commit mutation rolls back RESULT and leaves BEGIN pending; a post-commit mutation suppresses acknowledgment while keeping the committed result. Original-lease checks, revocation, unknown-outcome handling and all existing GET/session/database/cooldown bounds remain. New v3 events alone allow at most1536 encoded bytes; earlier events retain1024. The4MiB database,256-session cap, four-GET/20-second session, original copied rows and hash-chain formats otherwise remain. Older app readers may refuse v3 events; preserve private backups and review downgrade compatibility. The private encrypted bundle envelope/KDF remains unchanged, and no import/repair is introduced.

Regression covers self-consistent replacement inventory reports/exports, changed asset rules, changed quote values/source/account digest before retention, copied/live/encrypted changed observations, v2 nonpromotion, incomplete/tampered projections, larger valid v3 events, five real injected writers, commit-boundary mutation and cold/history preservation. Mandatory typed-read, encrypted quote and process-death profiles require normalized binding and legacy noninvention. Fixture data and prior published reports stay distinct from accepted real-account evidence. No UI/control/API/tool/default connection, model/order/ledger authority, funding or cloud deployment is added; beginner tabs and black/bright-purple bottom-right help remain.

Next critical integration work is a complete centrally budgeted mixed-operation model and reviewed actual broker-paper/source/clock/event/fee/hold/funds acceptance, followed by sustained public-feed paper trials and independent review. Secure provider-held funding, governed live and computer-off cloud operation, native profile/ACL and explicit owner1.0 approval remain open. ## v0.169 read-only complete local mixed-history review

The new internal paper_read_review.review function compares supplied normalized observations against every retained session in one explicit, fully replayed PaperReadPacing journal. Callers pin the expected current head. Supplied bindings are frozen before journal callbacks and keyed by unique completed heads; an omitted successful session, unknown extra binding, changed result, unbound old receipt, legacy purpose or failed/pending/interrupted tail withholds full local consistency. All five purposes must have matched results before complete_mixed_history_consistent is true. Individual-purpose consistency and five-purpose coverage remain distinct; status stays REVIEW_REQUIRED and all provider/account/funds/routing/release authority flags stay false.

Fixed inventory/asset/quote scope uses existing exact endpoint checks and declared account scope. Recovery reconstructs only the selected original-ID GET and checks the normalized journal identity. Activity scope requires the canonical collection receipt payload, re-normalizes retained order/activity fields, revalidates page/content/timing/cursor metadata and reconstructs exact creation-window/page targets. Its payload identity must match the fingerprint already bound in the successful pacing result. These remain co-reported cooperating-code checks, not a trusted mapping from raw response bytes to normalization, source identity, complete coverage or event/clock/fee/hold/funds acceptance.

The review returns only aggregate counts, fixed reasons, declared operation counts and account/head hashes. It performs no vault/network access, ownership-file creation, database initialization/writes, import, migration, repair, retry, deletion or execution. Missing state remains missing. A second full replay sample catches an append during composition; this is not an atomic reservation, a distributed fence or protection against valid-tail deletion/ABA. Earlier rows and published reports are preserved. No GUI/control/API/private executable command or default connection is added; beginner setup and black/bright-purple bottom-right help remain.

Inputs are bounded to256 bindings,2MiB total canonical bytes,256KiB per binding, depth32 and131072 structural nodes. Malformed/duplicate/nonfinite/cyclic or over-limit inputs fail with fixed codes. Larger legitimate histories may require future bounded streaming review; never delete, slice or substitute journals to bypass a cap or omit uncertainty. The current function consumes supplied private observations and does not create a new durable result-retention service or prove those observations were retained at original receipt time. Provider-wide coordination and historical operational acceptance remain open.

Regression exercises five actual bounded injected readers, exact scope and projection disagreements, old profiles and uncertain later tails, frozen input and changed-head races, private fixed failures, independent byte/structural caps and cold-process review with network/vault seams forbidden. The mandatory mixed-reader offline profile now additionally requires this complete-history review fixture, including a100-row activity page followed by an empty second page, changed-result/omission/legacy-tail refusal and unchanged files. No actual provider, account, OS credential or market acceptance follows.

Next: durable mixed-operation evidence retention and centrally/provider-wide budget coordination with authorized broker-paper/native-profile trials, then actual source/clock/event/fee/hold/funds and sustained-market acceptance. ## v0.169.1 content-free durable mixed-review receipts

A complete v0.169 mixed-read review may now be retained in a separate explicitly selected private SQLite journal. Store only the account digest, exact pacing head, aggregate review digest, a manifest of per-binding content digests, bounded operation counts and completeness booleans. Never retain normalized observations, dynamic request scopes, credentials, provider payloads or private account identifiers in this journal. It is local review evidence, not a substitute for the original private observations.

Require full hash-chain replay, exact expected store head, the unchanged complete pacing review and an independent caller revocation check before and during commit. Recheck both after commit; a changed input loses acknowledgment but must not erase or repair the committed boundary. Exact duplicates are idempotent. Reject pacing database aliases, SQLite sidecars and ownership-lock collisions before file creation. Missing, partial, failed, interrupted, legacy or changed review input must not initialize storage.

Keep every authority flag false. A receipt does not authenticate response bytes or provider identity, coordinate provider-wide quotas, prevent valid-tail deletion, accept a broker account, reconcile funds or authorize routing/release. No UI/control, automatic read/retry or default path exists, so existing beginner tabs and black/bright-purple hover help remain unchanged. Next work is provider-wide budget ownership plus authorized native paper-account review and sustained real-feed evidence.

## v0.170.0 local provider-scope paper-read budget

All five typed paper GET purposes may opt into one `PaperProviderReadBudget` shared across account-bound pacing journals. Acquire its ownership before account pacing ownership and hold both through provider IO and both durable completions. The provider journal reserves the maximum GET count for the declared purpose, stores only provider/account digests, operation/count/outcome metadata and exact before/after account-pacing heads, and enforces one local cooldown. Known account-local blocks are checked before provider reservation, but a later race, crash or account/provider commit failure must leave a provider pending unknown. Never delete or auto-release it; only the explicit exact-head interruption records unknown outcome and a full-day cooldown.

Saved-key readers preflight the exact provider-budget head before opening the vault. Provider budget, account pacing, request/activity retention and every SQLite companion/ownership path must remain distinct. ## v0.172.3 independently budgeted Windows safety gates

The Windows safety workflow now runs the complete Python regression, bounded source-fingerprinted release trials with saved-evidence revalidation, and rendered-browser control-help acceptance as three separate jobs. Each job performs its own pinned checkout and dependency setup, uses nonpersistent checkout credentials and has an independent timeout: 25 minutes for regression and 15 minutes each for release evidence and browser acceptance. Release and browser artifacts still upload on failure. A regression timeout or failure can no longer consume the evidence/browser budget, and a browser installation failure cannot erase release-trial execution.

The cancelled safety run is not relabeled as accepted; v0.172.3 must prove all three new jobs independently.

## v0.173.0 provider and normalized-result composition

`paper_provider_result_review.review` joins the complete exact-head provider/account history review with each account's normalized-result and request-scope review. Inputs are explicitly supplied, structurally bounded, frozen before journal callbacks and capped at 2 MiB in aggregate across at most six accounts. Every retained session must match; five-purpose coverage is global, while individual account subsets remain subject to their own exact bindings. A second full provider/account review rejects changes during composition. Matching provider counts alone never establish result consistency. The sampled reviews do not reserve admission or provide cross-database atomicity.

Optional `retain_review` uses the existing bounded 256-receipt/4 MiB journal with a distinct `nexus.paper-provider-result-review-receipt.v1` profile. It retains only digests, counts and coverage booleans, never normalized observations, targets, account IDs, order IDs, credentials or paths. Original count/history-only receipts remain readable under their original profile and gain no normalized-result claim. Older readers can reject the new profile: preserve an owner backup and use a compatible reader; do not rewrite or downgrade a mixed-profile journal. Exact source/store heads, path-family separation, repeated admission and revocation checks remain required. Revocation after commit reports lost acknowledgment without erasing the committed receipt.

The mandatory offline pacing profile now requires the actual five-reader injected provider/result fixture, including paginated activity, altered/missing-result refusal, receipt replay, content suppression and source preservation. Regression covers all five request-target substitutions, two actual account histories, swapped evidence, stale heads, concurrent append, frozen inputs, strict profile fields and pre/post-commit revocation. These are temporary injected observations and local consistency checks, not actual provider access, raw-byte normalization proof, source/clock/quota/fee/hold/funds attestation, reconciled broker acceptance or routing authority. No GUI, API, default connection, credential enrollment, scheduler, retry, order, funding, live/cloud or release permission is added. Existing beginner controls/help remain unchanged.

The next operational gates remain explicit broker-paper/native-profile acceptance, sustained real-feed trials, full source/clock/event/fee/hold/funds reconciliation, secure provider-held funding and separately governed live operation, computer-off cloud acceptance and independent/owner release signoff.
